Alternative use memory loads from operand variables rather than persistent registers. For each asm operation, we can use operand "m" for A pointer variable (the function argument) to load into register when needed. That frees registers for pointers but need address of pointer variable on stack; the asm string can use `%[Aptr]` memory operand. Example:
```
void *aptr;
asm ("mov %[Aptr], %%bx" : : [Aptr] "m" (A) : "bx");
```
This will expand to `mov -2(%bp), %bx` or something. We can use this in loops? Yes, we can load A base from stack memory each outer iteration. But the memory operand (`A` variable) is not modified. __asm__ __volatile__ (
    "mov %[y], %%di\n\t"
    "mov %[n], %%cx\n\t"
    "test %%cx, %%cx\n\t"
    "jz 2f\n\t"
    "1:\n\t"
    "fldz\n\t"
    "fstpl (%%di)\n\t"
    "add $8, %%di\n\t"
    "dec %%cx\n\t"
    "jnz 1b\n\t"
    "2:\n\t"
    "xor %%ax, %%ax\n\t"
    "mov %[A], %%si\n\t"
    "mov %[stride], %%dx\n\t"
    "3:\n\t"
    "cmp %[m], %%ax\n\t"
    "jae 9f\n\t"
    "mov %%si, %%bx\n\t"
    "mov %[x], %%di\n\t"
    "mov %[n], %%cx\n\t"
    "fldz\n\t"
    "test %%cx, %%cx\n\t"
    "jz 5f\n\t"
    "4:\n\t"
    "fldl (%%si)\n\t"
    "fmull (%%di)\n\t"
    "faddp\n\t"
    "add $8, %%si\n\t"
    "add $8, %%di\n\t"
    "dec %%cx\n\t"
    "jnz 4b\n\t"
    "5:\n\t"
    "mov %[tmp], %%di\n\t"
    "mov %%ax, %%si\n\t"
    "shl $3, %%si\n\t"
    "add %%si, %%di\n\t"
    "fstl (%%di)\n\t"
    "mov %%bx, %%si\n\t"
    "mov %[y], %%di\n\t"
    "mov %[n], %%cx\n\t"
    "test %%cx, %%cx\n\t"
    "jz 7f\n\t"
    "6:\n\t"
    "fldl (%%si)\n\t"
    "fmul %%st(1), %%st(0)\n\t"
    "faddl (%%di)\n\t"
    "fstpl (%%di)\n\t"
    "add $8, %%si\n\t"
    "add $8, %%di\n\t"
    "dec %%cx\n\t"
    "jnz 6b\n\t"
    "7:\n\t"
    "fstp %%st(0)\n\t"
    "add %[stride], %%bx\n\t"
    "mov %%bx, %%si\n\t"
    "inc %%ax\n\t"
    "jmp 3b\n\t"
    "9:\n\t"
    :
    : [m] "i" (_PB_M), [n] "i" (_PB_N),
      [stride] "i" (_PB_N * (long)sizeof(DATA_TYPE)),
      [A] "m" (A), [x] "m" (x), [y] "m" (y), [tmp] "m" (tmp)

);

    "xor %%ax, %%ax\n\t"
    "mov %[A], %%si\n\t"
    "mov %[stride], %%dx\n\t"
    "3:\n\t"
    "cmp %[m], %%ax\n\t"
    "jae 9f\n\t"
    "mov %%si, %%bx\n\t"
    "mov %[x], %%di\n\t"
    "mov %[n], %%cx\n\t"
    "fldz\n\t"
    "test %%cx, %%cx\n\t"
    "jz 5f\n\t"
    "4:\n\t"
    "fldl (%%si)\n\t"
    "fmull (%%di)\n\t"
    "faddp\n\t"
    "add $8, %%si\n\t"
    "add $8, %%di\n\t"
    "dec %%cx\n\t"
    "jnz 4b\n\t"
    "5:\n\t"
    "mov %[tmp], %%di\n\t"
    "mov %%ax, %%si\n\t"
    "shl $3, %%si\n\t"
    "add %%si, %%di\n\t"
    "fstl (%%di)\n\t"
    "mov %%bx, %%si\n\t"
    "mov %[y], %%di\n\t"
    "mov %[n], %%cx\n\t"
    "test %%cx, %%cx\n\t"
    "jz 7f\n\t"
    "6:\n\t"
    "fldl (%%si)\n\t"
    "fmul %%st(1), %%st\n\t"
    "faddl (%%di)\n\t"
    "fstpl (%%di)\n\t"
    "add $8, %%si\n\t"
    "add $8, %%di\n\t"
    "dec %%cx\n\t"
    "jnz 6b\n\t"
    "7:\n\t"
    "fstp %%st(0)\n\t"
    "add %[stride], %%bx\n\t"
    "mov %%bx, %%si\n\t"
    "inc %%ax\n\t"
    "jmp 3b\n\t"
    "9:\n\t"
    :
    : [m] "i" (_PB_M),
      [n] "i" (_PB_N),
      [stride] "i" (_PB_N * sizeof(DATA_TYPE)),
      [A] "m" (A),
      [x] "m" (x),
      [y] "m" (y),
      [tmp] "m" (tmp)
    : "ax", "bx", "cx", "dx", "si", "di", "cc", "memory"
);
```

Potential hidden issue: `fmul %%st(1), %%st` in AT&T: In GAS, order for x87 is reversed relative to source,dest? Let's confirm absolutely with examples from GNU as manual.

```
This is "add st(1) to st" or "add st to st(1)"? It says "The direction of the source and destination in floating-point operations is the opposite of the direction in integer operations." Wait, I recall something: For x87 instructions, GAS `fadd %st, %st(1)` actually adds `st` to `st(1)` (destination first?)? Let's recall from GAS docs:
"Some machine instructions have the operands in a different order than the Intel syntax. In AT&T, the source is the first operand, destination second. For example, `fsub %st, %st(1)` does `st(1) = st(1) - st`; `fsubr %st, %st(1)` does `st(1) = st - st(1)`."
I think this is source,dest. But I have also seen GAS x87 uses "reverse" because the Intel `FADD ST(1), ST` would be written `fadd %st, %st(1)`? Let's check with known objdump outputs.

If you compile:
```

```
What bytes? I can infer from encoding.
- `D8 C1` disassembled by Intel as `FADD ST(0), ST(1)`: ST0 = ST0 + ST1.
- In AT&T objdump, `D8 C1` is displayed as `fadd %st(1), %st`? I think yes. Because AT&T register order source,dest? Actually `D8 C1` has Intel dest ST0, source ST1; in AT&T, source ST1, dest ST0 -> `fadd %st(1), %st`. So `fadd %st(1), %st` computes ST0 = ST0 + ST1. Good. Thus `fmul %st(1), %st` -> ST0 = ST0 * ST1. Perfect.
And `DC C1` (Intel FADD ST(1), ST(0); ST1 = ST1 + ST0) is displayed as `fadd %st, %st(1)`. So our assumption consistent. Good.

`faddp` no operands corresponds to Intel `FADDP ST(1), ST` which is ST1=ST1+ST0; pop. AT&T no operands likely `faddp %st, %st(1)`? But no operands defaults to that. Good.

Let's write final code now mentally with modifications:
```
__asm__ __volatile__ (
    "mov %[y], %%di\n\t"
    "mov %[n], %%cx\n\t"
    "test %%cx, %%cx\n\t"
    "jz 2f\n\t"
    "1:\n\t"
    "fldz\n\t"
    "fstpl (%%di)\n\t"
    "add $8, %%di\n\t"
    "dec %%cx\n\t"
    "jnz 1b\n\t"
    "2:\n\t"
    "xor %%ax, %%ax\n\t"
    "mov %[A], %%si\n\t"
    "mov %[stride], %%dx\n\t"
    "3:\n\t"
    "cmp %[m], %%ax\n\t"
    "jae 9f\n\t"
    "mov %%si, %%bx\n\t"
    "mov %[x], %%di\n\t"
    "mov %[n], %%cx\n\t"
    "fldz\n\t"
    "test %%cx, %%cx\n\t"
    "jz 5f\n\t"
    "4:\n\t"
    "fldl (%%si)\n\t"
    "fmull (%%di)\n\t"
    "faddp\n\t"
    "add $8, %%si\n\t"
    "add $8, %%di\n\t"
    "dec %%cx\n\t"
    "jnz 4b\n\t"
    "5:\n\t"
    "mov %[tmp], %%di\n\t"
    "mov %%ax, %%si\n\t"
    "add %%si, %%si\n\t"
    "add %%si, %%si\n\t"
    "add %%si, %%si\n\t"
    "add %%si, %%di\n\t"
    "fstl (%%di)\n\t"
    "mov %%bx, %%si\n\t"
    "mov %[y], %%di\n\t"
    "mov %[n], %%cx\n\t"
    "test %%cx, %%cx\n\t"
    "jz 7f\n\t"
    "6:\n\t"
    "fldl (%%si)\n\t"
    "fmul %%st(1), %%st\n\t"
    "faddl (%%di)\n\t"
    "fstpl (%%di)\n\t"
    "add $8, %%si\n\t"
    "add $8, %%di\n\t"
    "dec %%cx\n\t"
    "jnz 6b\n\t"
    "7:\n\t"
    "fstp %%st(0)\n\t"
    "add %[stride], %%bx\n\t"
    "mov %%bx, %%si\n\t"
    "inc %%ax\n\t"
    "jmp 3b\n\t"
    "9:\n\t"
    :
    : [m] "i" (_PB_M),
      [n] "i" (_PB_N),
      [stride] "i" (_PB_N * sizeof(DATA_TYPE)),
      [A] "m" (A),
      [x] "m" (x),
      [y] "m" (y),
      [tmp] "m" (tmp)
    : "ax", "bx", "cx", "dx", "si", "di", "cc", "memory"
);
```

We have after `fmull`: ST0=product, ST1=sum.
Intel `FADDP ST(1), ST(0)` = ST1 = ST1 + ST0; pop. GAS no-operand `faddp` = same. Then stack top after pop is old ST1 (which was sum). Wait pop removes ST0, and ST1 becomes top. The value ST0 after pop is original ST1 = sum? Let's carefully simulate stack with x87 "top" notation:

After fldl A: stack grows downward? In AT&T, top is ST0; pushing moves old ST0 to ST1. So before faddp: ST0=product, ST1=sum. Instruction FADDP ST1, ST0: ST1 = ST1 + ST0 = sum+product; pop -> The stack pointer increments, so the old ST1 becomes new ST0. Therefore new ST0 = sum+product. Correct. Good.

Final code with no stride:
```
__asm__ __volatile__ (
    "mov %[y], %%di\n\t"
    "mov %[n], %%cx\n\t"
    "test %%cx, %%cx\n\t"
    "jz 2f\n\t"
    "1:\n\t"
    "fldz\n\t"
    "fstpl (%%di)\n\t"
    "add $8, %%di\n\t"
    "dec %%cx\n\t"
    "jnz 1b\n\t"
    "2:\n\t"
    "xor %%ax, %%ax\n\t"
    "mov %[A], %%si\n\t"
    "3:\n\t"
    "cmp %[m], %%ax\n\t"
    "jae 9f\n\t"
    "mov %%si, %%bx\n\t"
    "mov %[x], %%di\n\t"
    "mov %[n], %%cx\n\t"
    "fldz\n\t"
    "test %%cx, %%cx\n\t"
    "jz 5f\n\t"
    "4:\n\t"
    "fldl (%%si)\n\t"
    "fmull (%%di)\n\t"
    "faddp\n\t"
    "add $8, %%si\n\t"
    "add $8, %%di\n\t"
    "dec %%cx\n\t"
    "jnz 4b\n\t"
    "5:\n\t"
    "mov %[tmp], %%di\n\t"
    "mov %%ax, %%si\n\t"
    "add %%si, %%si\n\t"
    "add %%si, %%si\n\t"
    "add %%si, %%si\n\t"
    "add %%si, %%di\n\t"
    "fstl (%%di)\n\t"
    "mov %%bx, %%si\n\t"
    "mov %[y], %%di\n\t"
    "mov %[n], %%cx\n\t"
    "test %%cx, %%cx\n\t"
    "jz 7f\n\t"
    "6:\n\t"
    "fldl (%%si)\n\t"
    "fmul %%st(1), %%st\n\t"
    "faddl (%%di)\n\t"
    "fstpl (%%di)\n\t"
    "add $8, %%si\n\t"
    "add $8, %%di\n\t"
    "dec %%cx\n\t"
    "jnz 6b\n\t"
    "7:\n\t"
    "fstp %%st(0)\n\t"
    "inc %%ax\n\t"
    "jmp 3b\n\t"
    "9:\n\t"
    :
    : [m] "i" (_PB_M), [n] "i" (_PB_N),
      [A] "m" (A), [x] "m" (x), [y] "m" (y), [tmp] "m" (tmp)
    : "ax", "bx", "cx", "si", "di", "cc", "memory"
